Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, bgm.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_27x27 (precision INT27)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[27], ay[27], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[27]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[27]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The design

Baseline: the unmodified bgm under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, median of five seeds, 1 to 5) has a worst register-to-register path of 19.5909 ns on its one clock clock (I/O pads not timed) and uses 11 DSP tiles (as packed at seed 1) of the device's 132. Its used logic-block area is 6.35391e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905) and its routed wirelength is 395851, so its area x delay x wirelength product, the objective, is 4.92751e+14. Its slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report) is in a module not established (no multiply on the path): no multiply, a register through 21 LUTs and three runs of adder cells (1, 7 and 11) into a flip-flop with synchronous reset; the largest wire into one cell 1.18 ns. The next slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report), in a module not established (no multiply on the path), is no multiply, a register through 22 LUTs and three runs of adder cells (1, 5 and 22) into a flip-flop with synchronous reset, 19.652 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

A row marked "on this design" is measured on the unmodified design's own placement under this run's VPR settings, with the design's hard multiplier standing in for the block; the other rows are sampled over earlier runs of other designs on this architecture.

Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

idoperationinstanceswhat it already is in the baselineregister stages between the products and their sumproducts in the whole sum this one belongs to
mul_r2.$mul$bgm.v:1174$336(sum_in=fpu_mul)mult_add11on a DSP tile in one_mult_27x27 mode, one to a tile on all 11 tilesproducts registered 2 stage(s) before the sumthe only product in its sum
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Cost (guidance: go against it only with a reason in the plan)
  • The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage (the logic between one register and the next). A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage should not be expected to make the design faster, and should not make another stage slower than it.
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • Moving or adding registers changes stage lengths and when values arrive.
  • Area falls when soft adders and flip-flops move into blocks.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone. Synthesis may already have placed the design's multiplies on tiles, so a block that only replaces one saves little: count tiles against the baseline.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
Objective

Minimize area × delay × wirelength against the baseline. If you expect no rewrite to lower it, output TIER: NONE. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: No rewrite: no candidate can be rewritten correctly.
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
TILES: <how many DSP tiles the whole design then uses, one integer>
STAGE_NS: <the delay of the slowest stage the rewrite leaves, in ns, one number>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • A reviewer checks the plan automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.

assign prod = result[47:0];
endmodule
  1. bgm.v:695 becomes mul_r2 u5(.clk(clk), .clr(~fpu_op[1]), .opa(fracta_mul), .opb(fractb_mul), .prod(prod));
  2. bgm.v:734 becomes assign or_result = mul_00 | div_00 | ~fpu_op[1];

Why this is the same behaviour.

Latency. In the original the chain from the module input opa is opa_r (bgm.v:597-598) -> prod1 (1174) -> prod (1177): three register edges, with fracta_mul combinational from opa_r. After the rewrite the chain is opa_r -> block operand register -> block result register: the block's two registers stand exactly where prod1 and prod stood, the operands presented to the block are the same fracta_mul/fractb_mul on the same edges, so prod after edge k is the product of fracta_mul,fractb_mul sampled before edge k-1, as before. Both operands are unsigned 24-bit wires zero-extended into the 27-bit ports, the 48-bit product is exact and never reaches result[48], so result[47:0] is bit-for-bit the original opa*opb. No term of the original arithmetic is dropped: bx and chainin have no counterpart and are tied to 0, as the block's contract allows.

Start-up. The original's prod is 0 after edge 1 (it loads prod1's power-up 0) and 0 after edge 2 (prod1 loaded fracta_mul*fractb_mul with opa_r=opb_r=0, and fracta_mul = {!expa_dn, opa_r[22:0]} = 0 for a zero operand, bgm.v:1093/1102). fpu_op is 0 before the first edge and 3'b010 after every edge (bgm.v:593-595), so ~fpu_op[1] is high only at edge 1: the block clears all of its registers there, giving result=0 after edge 1, and after edge 2 it loads the product of the cleared operand registers, 0 — the same two zeros. From edge 3 on the block is in its steady state and matches the original. Nothing relies on a power-up value of a register the rewrite adds; the rewrite adds no register.

The one X window. Between time 0 and the first clock edge the block's result is X (the block has no initial value), whereas the original's prod is 0 there, and that window is sampled once, at edge 1, by out_o1, ine_o1, overflow_o1, inf_o1 and zero_o1 through post_norm. Forcing or_result (i.e. post_norm.output_zero) high in exactly that window reproduces the original's values there, without any gate in the 48-bit datapath: with the design's registers at their power-up 0, post_norm sees fpu_op=0, exp_in=0, exp_ovf=0, rmode=0, opa_dn=opb_dn=0, so dn=1 (bgm.v:1330, exp_in_00=1), op_mul=op_div=op_f2i=op_i2f=0 and max_num=0 (bgm.v:1550, rmode_00=1). In the original with fract_in=0 this yields out=31'h0 (shift left by 2 of zero, no rounding, exp_out=0), ine=0, overflow=0, underflow=0; in the rewrite with fract_in=X and output_zero=1, exp_out_final and fract_out_final are forced to 0 (bgm.v:1596/1600) so out=31'h0 and out_d_00=1, ine reduces to max_num=0 (bgm.v:1672-1674, dn=1 kills the r/s terms), overflow = ovf0 | ovf1 = 0 (exp_out_final=0; ovf1 is ANDed with !dn=0) and underflow = (!fract_in[47] & exp_out1_co) & !dn = 0. Every value the five registers latch at edge 1 is therefore identical to the original's. The other reader of prod, bgm.v:843, is ANDed with (opa_dn | opb_dn) = 0 in that window, so it is 0 in both. From edge 1 on result is defined, ~fpu_op[1] is 0 and or_result is the original expression, so no later cycle is touched. bgm's reset port is unused by the design (only bgm.v:6/20), so the block's reset is never asserted again and the testbench's mid-run resets change nothing.

Blocks and tiles. 11 blocks, one per fpu_mul, each taking a DSP tile alone: 11 DSP tiles, the same count as the baseline, which already placed these 11 multiplies on tiles. The gain is not the tile count but the 1056 soft flip-flops (96 per instance: prod1 and prod) that the block's two register stages absorb, about 106 BLEs' worth per instance.

Stages and delay. No block is chained to another: the longest series of blocks in any stage is one, and a stage that lies inside a block is 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, far under the critical path. Two stages change:

  • The stage ending at the old prod1 (opa_r -> fracta_mul -> adder-tree multiply -> flip-flop) is replaced by opa_r -> fracta_mul -> block input register, ~0.06 + wire + 0.019 setup; it was not the slowest stage and is now much shorter.
  • The slowest stage of the design is unchanged in content: prod -> post_norm (pri_encoder, the left shifter, the fract_out+1 round adder, bgm.v:1300/1405/1339) -> out_o1, 19.5909 ns in the baseline and 19.684 ns as measured on the kept candidate, where it started at the soft prod flip-flop (clock-to-Q 0.060 plus a 0.660 ns wire). It now starts at the block's result register instead: 0.060 clock-to-Q plus a "block into soft logic" wire (0.54-1.90, median 0.96), i.e. about +0.3 ns at the median, giving ~19.9 ns. That is the STAGE_NS above; it is the slowest stage, and the rewrite makes no other stage longer than it. This is the one place the plan goes against the cost guidance: it lengthens the slowest stage by roughly 1.5% to take 1056 flip-flops (about -2.9% of used logic area on top of the -2.85% already measured for the first pass) out of the design, and it drops the first pass's extra routing of the raw 32-bit opa/opb into the DSP column (the first pass's +1.43% wirelength) by feeding the blocks from the local fracta_mul/fractb_mul again. At the median wire the product lands near 0.94 x 1.02 x ~1.01 ~ 0.97 of the baseline, below the kept 0.990.

Soft logic leaving each stage. Out of the fracta_mul -> prod1 stage: the 24x24 multiply (it leaves the one_mult_27x27 DSP for the block) and the 48-bit prod1 register. Out of the prod1 -> prod stage: that stage disappears entirely with the 48-bit prod register. Added soft logic: one extra literal (~fpu_op[1]) on the existing or_result LUT and one reset net per block; nothing is added to the 48-bit product path or to the slowest stage.

Register stages, before and after. Before: operands fracta_mul/fractb_mul to the product prod = 2 register stages, both soft (prod1, prod), and prod is read combinationally by fract_denorm/post_norm in the next stage. After: the same 2 register stages, both inside the block (operand register, result register), 0 soft; the product is read directly off result[47:0] in the same cycle the original read prod. Latency from opa/opb to prod is 3 edges before and after.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the design's slowest stage is post_norm's normalize-round cone (leading-zero count, 48-bit shift, 24-bit round adder, bgm.v:1300-1484), which contains no multiply and cannot be moved into the block; inserting one pipeline register inside post_norm (e.g. between the shifter and the rounding adder) in all 11 fpu_mul and 9 fpu_add instances would roughly halve the period, after which the block's own 2.219 ns path and the DSP-to-logic wire would still be slack-free and the area won here would convert into a product gain several times larger.

1	// mult_add_int_27x27: result = ax*ay + bx + chainin, unsigned, the product and bx zero-extended
2	// to 64 bits, modulo 2^64 (the sum wraps at the result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[27], ay[27], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[27] in;
4	// result[64], chainout[64], scanout[27] out. mode_sigs and scanin are not read (the scan chain is
5	// unused and tied to 0 in every spec). bx is 36 bits, the pb_type's port width (the arch header's
6	// "27x27 plus 64" names an addend the tile's data pins cannot carry).
7	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
8	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx and chainin alike); the product and
9	// the sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
10	// Cycles: ax, ay, bx and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
11	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (1 cycle from the
12	// inputs).
13	// scanout is the ay register (ay to scanout 1), as in mult_add_int_18x19; nothing specifies it.
14	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
15	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
16	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
17	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
18	// X until the first clear.
19	module mult_add_int_27x27 (
20	    input clk,
21	    input reset,
22	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
23	    input [26:0] ax,
24	    input [26:0] ay,
25	    input [35:0] bx,
26	    input [63:0] chainin,
27	    input [26:0] scanin,
28	    output reg [63:0] result,
29	    output [63:0] chainout,
30	    output [26:0] scanout
31	);
32	
33	    // stage 1: the input registers
34	    reg [26:0] ax_reg;
35	    reg [26:0] ay_reg;
36	    reg [35:0] bx_reg;
37	    reg [63:0] chainin_reg;
38	
39	    // combinational middle: the product, the addend and chainin, from the registers
40	    wire [53:0] prod = ax_reg * ay_reg;                          // 27x27 -> 54, exact
41	    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx_reg + chainin_reg;              // zero-extended, modulo 2^64
42	
43	    always @(posedge clk) begin
44	        if (reset) begin
45	            result <= 0;
46	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
47	        end
48	        else begin
49	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; chainin_reg <= chainin;
50	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
51	        end
52	    end
53	
54	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
55	    assign scanout  = ay_reg;
56	
57	endmodule

prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_27x27 (precision INT27)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[27], ay[27], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[27]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[27]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The design

The design source is bgm.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

idoperationinstanceswhat it already is in the baselineregister stages between the products and their sumproducts in the whole sum this one belongs to
mul_r2.$mul$bgm.v:1174$336(sum_in=fpu_mul)mult_add11on a DSP tile in one_mult_27x27 mode, one to a tile on all 11 tilesproducts registered 2 stage(s) before the sumthe only product in its sum
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.

The plan

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: mul_r2.$mul$bgm.v:1174$336(sum_in=fpu_mul)
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 11
TILES: 11
STAGE_NS: 19.9
MAPPING: per block (one per mul_r2 instance u5, i.e. one per fpu_mul, 11 of them): clk <- clk (bgm's single clock, bgm.v:101 etc.); reset <- clr, a new 1-bit input port of mul_r2 driven in fpu_mul by ~fpu_op[1] (fpu_op is the design's own register, bgm.v:536/593-595); mode_sigs <- 12'b0; ax <- {3'b0, opa} where mul_r2.opa keeps its existing driver fracta_mul (pre_norm_fmul's combinational fraction output, bgm.v:666/695/1100-1103); ay <- {3'b0, opb} = {3'b0, fractb_mul}; bx <- 36'b0; chainin <- 64'b0; scanin <- 27'b0; result[47:0] -> prod, by assign prod = result[47:0] (both soft registers prod1 and prod of bgm.v:1171-1177 are removed; prod becomes a wire); result[63:48] unused (a 24x24 product never reaches bit 48); chainout, scanout left unconnected. prod keeps driving fract_denorm (bgm.v:719-720) and (prod!=48'b0) (bgm.v:843) unchanged; in fpu_mul, or_result becomes mul_00 | div_00 | ~fpu_op[1] (bgm.v:734).

PLAN:

Candidate regions. There is one candidate, mul_r2.$mul$bgm.v:1174$336(sum_in=fpu_mul), 11 instances. It is converted; nothing is left.

What changes against the unmodified source (this plan is applied to bgm.v as given, not to the kept candidate).

  1. mul_r2 (bgm.v:1166-1179) keeps its 24-bit opa/opb ports and its instantiation operands (fracta_mul, fractb_mul), gains one input clr, and loses both always @(posedge clk) blocks and the reg [47:0] prod1, prod:
module mul_r2(clk, clr, opa, opb, prod);
input		clk;
input		clr;			// clears the block at the first clock edge
input	[23:0]	opa, opb;
output	[47:0]	prod;

wire	[63:0]	result;

mult_add_int_27x27 u0(
		.clk(clk), .reset(clr), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
		.ax({3'b0, opa}), .ay({3'b0, opb}), .bx(36'b0),
		.chainin(64'b0), .scanin(27'b0),
		.result(result), .chainout(), .scanout()
		);

assign prod = result[47:0];
endmodule
  1. bgm.v:695 becomes mul_r2 u5(.clk(clk), .clr(~fpu_op[1]), .opa(fracta_mul), .opb(fractb_mul), .prod(prod));
  2. bgm.v:734 becomes assign or_result = mul_00 | div_00 | ~fpu_op[1];

Why this is the same behaviour.

Latency. In the original the chain from the module input opa is opa_r (bgm.v:597-598) -> prod1 (1174) -> prod (1177): three register edges, with fracta_mul combinational from opa_r. After the rewrite the chain is opa_r -> block operand register -> block result register: the block's two registers stand exactly where prod1 and prod stood, the operands presented to the block are the same fracta_mul/fractb_mul on the same edges, so prod after edge k is the product of fracta_mul,fractb_mul sampled before edge k-1, as before. Both operands are unsigned 24-bit wires zero-extended into the 27-bit ports, the 48-bit product is exact and never reaches result[48], so result[47:0] is bit-for-bit the original opa*opb. No term of the original arithmetic is dropped: bx and chainin have no counterpart and are tied to 0, as the block's contract allows.

Start-up. The original's prod is 0 after edge 1 (it loads prod1's power-up 0) and 0 after edge 2 (prod1 loaded fracta_mul*fractb_mul with opa_r=opb_r=0, and fracta_mul = {!expa_dn, opa_r[22:0]} = 0 for a zero operand, bgm.v:1093/1102). fpu_op is 0 before the first edge and 3'b010 after every edge (bgm.v:593-595), so ~fpu_op[1] is high only at edge 1: the block clears all of its registers there, giving result=0 after edge 1, and after edge 2 it loads the product of the cleared operand registers, 0 — the same two zeros. From edge 3 on the block is in its steady state and matches the original. Nothing relies on a power-up value of a register the rewrite adds; the rewrite adds no register.

The one X window. Between time 0 and the first clock edge the block's result is X (the block has no initial value), whereas the original's prod is 0 there, and that window is sampled once, at edge 1, by out_o1, ine_o1, overflow_o1, inf_o1 and zero_o1 through post_norm. Forcing or_result (i.e. post_norm.output_zero) high in exactly that window reproduces the original's values there, without any gate in the 48-bit datapath: with the design's registers at their power-up 0, post_norm sees fpu_op=0, exp_in=0, exp_ovf=0, rmode=0, opa_dn=opb_dn=0, so dn=1 (bgm.v:1330, exp_in_00=1), op_mul=op_div=op_f2i=op_i2f=0 and max_num=0 (bgm.v:1550, rmode_00=1). In the original with fract_in=0 this yields out=31'h0 (shift left by 2 of zero, no rounding, exp_out=0), ine=0, overflow=0, underflow=0; in the rewrite with fract_in=X and output_zero=1, exp_out_final and fract_out_final are forced to 0 (bgm.v:1596/1600) so out=31'h0 and out_d_00=1, ine reduces to max_num=0 (bgm.v:1672-1674, dn=1 kills the r/s terms), overflow = ovf0 | ovf1 = 0 (exp_out_final=0; ovf1 is ANDed with !dn=0) and underflow = (!fract_in[47] & exp_out1_co) & !dn = 0. Every value the five registers latch at edge 1 is therefore identical to the original's. The other reader of prod, bgm.v:843, is ANDed with (opa_dn | opb_dn) = 0 in that window, so it is 0 in both. From edge 1 on result is defined, ~fpu_op[1] is 0 and or_result is the original expression, so no later cycle is touched. bgm's reset port is unused by the design (only bgm.v:6/20), so the block's reset is never asserted again and the testbench's mid-run resets change nothing.

Blocks and tiles. 11 blocks, one per fpu_mul, each taking a DSP tile alone: 11 DSP tiles, the same count as the baseline, which already placed these 11 multiplies on tiles. The gain is not the tile count but the 1056 soft flip-flops (96 per instance: prod1 and prod) that the block's two register stages absorb, about 106 BLEs' worth per instance.

Stages and delay. No block is chained to another: the longest series of blocks in any stage is one, and a stage that lies inside a block is 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, far under the critical path. Two stages change:

  • The stage ending at the old prod1 (opa_r -> fracta_mul -> adder-tree multiply -> flip-flop) is replaced by opa_r -> fracta_mul -> block input register, ~0.06 + wire + 0.019 setup; it was not the slowest stage and is now much shorter.
  • The slowest stage of the design is unchanged in content: prod -> post_norm (pri_encoder, the left shifter, the fract_out+1 round adder, bgm.v:1300/1405/1339) -> out_o1, 19.5909 ns in the baseline and 19.684 ns as measured on the kept candidate, where it started at the soft prod flip-flop (clock-to-Q 0.060 plus a 0.660 ns wire). It now starts at the block's result register instead: 0.060 clock-to-Q plus a "block into soft logic" wire (0.54-1.90, median 0.96), i.e. about +0.3 ns at the median, giving ~19.9 ns. That is the STAGE_NS above; it is the slowest stage, and the rewrite makes no other stage longer than it. This is the one place the plan goes against the cost guidance: it lengthens the slowest stage by roughly 1.5% to take 1056 flip-flops (about -2.9% of used logic area on top of the -2.85% already measured for the first pass) out of the design, and it drops the first pass's extra routing of the raw 32-bit opa/opb into the DSP column (the first pass's +1.43% wirelength) by feeding the blocks from the local fracta_mul/fractb_mul again. At the median wire the product lands near 0.94 x 1.02 x ~1.01 ~ 0.97 of the baseline, below the kept 0.990.

Soft logic leaving each stage. Out of the fracta_mul -> prod1 stage: the 24x24 multiply (it leaves the one_mult_27x27 DSP for the block) and the 48-bit prod1 register. Out of the prod1 -> prod stage: that stage disappears entirely with the 48-bit prod register. Added soft logic: one extra literal (~fpu_op[1]) on the existing or_result LUT and one reset net per block; nothing is added to the 48-bit product path or to the slowest stage.

Register stages, before and after. Before: operands fracta_mul/fractb_mul to the product prod = 2 register stages, both soft (prod1, prod), and prod is read combinationally by fract_denorm/post_norm in the next stage. After: the same 2 register stages, both inside the block (operand register, result register), 0 soft; the product is read directly off result[47:0] in the same cycle the original read prod. Latency from opa/opb to prod is 3 edges before and after.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the design's slowest stage is post_norm's normalize-round cone (leading-zero count, 48-bit shift, 24-bit round adder, bgm.v:1300-1484), which contains no multiply and cannot be moved into the block; inserting one pipeline register inside post_norm (e.g. between the shifter and the rounding adder) in all 11 fpu_mul and 9 fpu_add instances would roughly halve the period, after which the block's own 2.219 ns path and the DSP-to-logic wire would still be slack-free and the area won here would convert into a product gain several times larger.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle. A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_bgm.v) and the block's behavioural model (mult_add_int_27x27.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: bgm.v

response

I'll read the design source and the reference files.

APPROVE

The plan is consistent with the source and the block contract on every point I checked:

  • Candidate / mapping. mul_r2 is instantiated only at bgm.v:695, once per fpu_mul, and bgm.v:99-316 contains exactly 11 fpu_mul (x0–x10) and 9 fpu_add (a0–a8), so 11 blocks / 11 tiles is right. All block inputs are connected, chainout/scanout unconnected, bx/chainin/scanin/mode_sigs tied to 0 as the contract allows; ax/ay are {3'b0, <24-bit>} and a 24x24 product never reaches bit 48, so result[47:0] is exactly opa*opb.
  • Latency. Original opa → opa_r (bgm.v:597) → prod1 (1174) → prod (1177) = 3 edges; after the rewrite opa_r → block operand register → block result register = 3 edges, with fracta_mul/fractb_mul combinational out of pre_norm_fmul (bgm.v:1047, 1100-1103, no reg). Steady state matches cycle for cycle.
  • Reset derivation. fpu_op (bgm.v:536, 593-595) is 0 only before the first edge and 3'b010 after every edge, so ~fpu_op[1] clears the block exactly once, at edge 1. Original prod is 0 after edges 1 and 2 (fracta_mul = {!expa_dn, opa_r[22:0]} = 0 for opa_r=0, bgm.v:1093/1102); the cleared block gives 0 after edges 1 and 2 as well. bgm's reset port appears only at lines 6 and 20 and drives nothing, so the testbench's mid-run resets (tb_equiv_bgm.v:126-134) are inert in both designs.
  • The pre-first-edge X window. With or_result = mul_00 | div_00 | ~fpu_op[1] (bgm.v:734), output_zero=1 forces fract_out_final/exp_out_final to 0 (1596/1600) → out_d=31'h0, matching the original's 31'h0 (dn=1 at 1330, shift-left-2 of zero, no rounding). ine collapses to max_num=0 (1550 with rmode_00=1; 1672-1674 with !dn=0), overflow=ovf0|ovf1=0, underflow=(…)&!dn=0, f2i_out_sign is independent of fract_in. Every register sampling that window — out_o1[30:0], ine_o1, overflow_o1, inf_o1, zero_o1 and also underflow_o1 — therefore latches the original values; the direct prod reader at bgm.v:843 is ANDed with sign=0 and with opa_dn|opb_dn, and opa_dn is a register (bgm.v:1031) zero at power-up, so the plan's stated reason holds. From edge 1 on ~fpu_op[1]=0 and or_result is the original expression.
  • Visibility and terms. prod's only readers are bgm.v:720 and bgm.v:843, both kept outside the block and fed from result[47:0]; prod1 has no other reader. No term is dropped.
  • Required items. The single candidate (converted, none left), 11 blocks / 11 tiles, longest block series of one at 2.219 ns against the ~19.9 ns slowest stage, the soft logic leaving the fracta_mul→prod1 and prod1→prod stages with both identified as not the slowest (the slowest being prod→post_norm→out_o1), and 2 register stages operand-to-result before (soft) and after (in-block) are all stated. No ASSUMES: is needed, and none is claimed.

Read completed /work/sim_reference/mult_add_int_27x27.v

{
 "file_path": "/work/sim_reference/mult_add_int_27x27.v"

output
1	// mult_add_int_27x27: result = ax*ay + bx + chainin, unsigned, the product and bx zero-extended
2	// to 64 bits, modulo 2^64 (the sum wraps at the result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[27], ay[27], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[27] in;
4	// result[64], chainout[64], scanout[27] out. mode_sigs and scanin are not read (the scan chain is
5	// unused and tied to 0 in every spec). bx is 36 bits, the pb_type's port width (the arch header's
6	// "27x27 plus 64" names an addend the tile's data pins cannot carry).
7	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
8	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx and chainin alike); the product and
9	// the sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
10	// Cycles: ax, ay, bx and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
11	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (1 cycle from the
12	// inputs).
13	// scanout is the ay register (ay to scanout 1), as in mult_add_int_18x19; nothing specifies it.
14	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
15	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
16	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
17	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
18	// X until the first clear.
19	module mult_add_int_27x27 (
20	    input clk,
21	    input reset,
22	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
23	    input [26:0] ax,
24	    input [26:0] ay,
25	    input [35:0] bx,
26	    input [63:0] chainin,
27	    input [26:0] scanin,
28	    output reg [63:0] result,
29	    output [63:0] chainout,
30	    output [26:0] scanout
31	);
32	
33	    // stage 1: the input registers
34	    reg [26:0] ax_reg;
35	    reg [26:0] ay_reg;
36	    reg [35:0] bx_reg;
37	    reg [63:0] chainin_reg;
38	
39	    // combinational middle: the product, the addend and chainin, from the registers
40	    wire [53:0] prod = ax_reg * ay_reg;                          // 27x27 -> 54, exact
41	    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx_reg + chainin_reg;              // zero-extended, modulo 2^64
42	
43	    always @(posedge clk) begin
44	        if (reset) begin
45	            result <= 0;
46	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
47	        end
48	        else begin
49	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; chainin_reg <= chainin;
50	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
51	        end
52	    end
53	
54	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
55	    assign scanout  = ay_reg;
56	
57	endmodule

prompt
Task

Edit bgm.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_27x27 (precision INT27)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[27], ay[27], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[27]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[27]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The design
  • Region in the approved plan: mul_r2.$mul$bgm.v:1174$336(sum_in=fpu_mul) (module mul_r2, operation mult_add, datatype int24). In the design its products are registered 2 stage(s) before the sum.
  • The sum these products feed also adds terms that are not products: the constant 1.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh bgm.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically and no person reads this, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: mul_r2.$mul$bgm.v:1174$336(sum_in=fpu_mul)
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 11
TILES: 11
STAGE_NS: 19.9
MAPPING: per block (one per mul_r2 instance u5, i.e. one per fpu_mul, 11 of them): clk <- clk (bgm's single clock, bgm.v:101 etc.); reset <- clr, a new 1-bit input port of mul_r2 driven in fpu_mul by ~fpu_op[1] (fpu_op is the design's own register, bgm.v:536/593-595); mode_sigs <- 12'b0; ax <- {3'b0, opa} where mul_r2.opa keeps its existing driver fracta_mul (pre_norm_fmul's combinational fraction output, bgm.v:666/695/1100-1103); ay <- {3'b0, opb} = {3'b0, fractb_mul}; bx <- 36'b0; chainin <- 64'b0; scanin <- 27'b0; result[47:0] -> prod, by assign prod = result[47:0] (both soft registers prod1 and prod of bgm.v:1171-1177 are removed; prod becomes a wire); result[63:48] unused (a 24x24 product never reaches bit 48); chainout, scanout left unconnected. prod keeps driving fract_denorm (bgm.v:719-720) and (prod!=48'b0) (bgm.v:843) unchanged; in fpu_mul, or_result becomes mul_00 | div_00 | ~fpu_op[1] (bgm.v:734).

PLAN:

Candidate regions. There is one candidate, mul_r2.$mul$bgm.v:1174$336(sum_in=fpu_mul), 11 instances. It is converted; nothing is left.

What changes against the unmodified source (this plan is applied to bgm.v as given, not to the kept candidate).

  1. mul_r2 (bgm.v:1166-1179) keeps its 24-bit opa/opb ports and its instantiation operands (fracta_mul, fractb_mul), gains one input clr, and loses both always @(posedge clk) blocks and the reg [47:0] prod1, prod:
module mul_r2(clk, clr, opa, opb, prod);
input		clk;
input		clr;			// clears the block at the first clock edge
input	[23:0]	opa, opb;
output	[47:0]	prod;

wire	[63:0]	result;

mult_add_int_27x27 u0(
		.clk(clk), .reset(clr), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
		.ax({3'b0, opa}), .ay({3'b0, opb}), .bx(36'b0),
		.chainin(64'b0), .scanin(27'b0),
		.result(result), .chainout(), .scanout()
		);

assign prod = result[47:0];
endmodule
  1. bgm.v:695 becomes mul_r2 u5(.clk(clk), .clr(~fpu_op[1]), .opa(fracta_mul), .opb(fractb_mul), .prod(prod));
  2. bgm.v:734 becomes assign or_result = mul_00 | div_00 | ~fpu_op[1];

Why this is the same behaviour.

Latency. In the original the chain from the module input opa is opa_r (bgm.v:597-598) -> prod1 (1174) -> prod (1177): three register edges, with fracta_mul combinational from opa_r. After the rewrite the chain is opa_r -> block operand register -> block result register: the block's two registers stand exactly where prod1 and prod stood, the operands presented to the block are the same fracta_mul/fractb_mul on the same edges, so prod after edge k is the product of fracta_mul,fractb_mul sampled before edge k-1, as before. Both operands are unsigned 24-bit wires zero-extended into the 27-bit ports, the 48-bit product is exact and never reaches result[48], so result[47:0] is bit-for-bit the original opa*opb. No term of the original arithmetic is dropped: bx and chainin have no counterpart and are tied to 0, as the block's contract allows.

Start-up. The original's prod is 0 after edge 1 (it loads prod1's power-up 0) and 0 after edge 2 (prod1 loaded fracta_mul*fractb_mul with opa_r=opb_r=0, and fracta_mul = {!expa_dn, opa_r[22:0]} = 0 for a zero operand, bgm.v:1093/1102). fpu_op is 0 before the first edge and 3'b010 after every edge (bgm.v:593-595), so ~fpu_op[1] is high only at edge 1: the block clears all of its registers there, giving result=0 after edge 1, and after edge 2 it loads the product of the cleared operand registers, 0 — the same two zeros. From edge 3 on the block is in its steady state and matches the original. Nothing relies on a power-up value of a register the rewrite adds; the rewrite adds no register.

The one X window. Between time 0 and the first clock edge the block's result is X (the block has no initial value), whereas the original's prod is 0 there, and that window is sampled once, at edge 1, by out_o1, ine_o1, overflow_o1, inf_o1 and zero_o1 through post_norm. Forcing or_result (i.e. post_norm.output_zero) high in exactly that window reproduces the original's values there, without any gate in the 48-bit datapath: with the design's registers at their power-up 0, post_norm sees fpu_op=0, exp_in=0, exp_ovf=0, rmode=0, opa_dn=opb_dn=0, so dn=1 (bgm.v:1330, exp_in_00=1), op_mul=op_div=op_f2i=op_i2f=0 and max_num=0 (bgm.v:1550, rmode_00=1). In the original with fract_in=0 this yields out=31'h0 (shift left by 2 of zero, no rounding, exp_out=0), ine=0, overflow=0, underflow=0; in the rewrite with fract_in=X and output_zero=1, exp_out_final and fract_out_final are forced to 0 (bgm.v:1596/1600) so out=31'h0 and out_d_00=1, ine reduces to max_num=0 (bgm.v:1672-1674, dn=1 kills the r/s terms), overflow = ovf0 | ovf1 = 0 (exp_out_final=0; ovf1 is ANDed with !dn=0) and underflow = (!fract_in[47] & exp_out1_co) & !dn = 0. Every value the five registers latch at edge 1 is therefore identical to the original's. The other reader of prod, bgm.v:843, is ANDed with (opa_dn | opb_dn) = 0 in that window, so it is 0 in both. From edge 1 on result is defined, ~fpu_op[1] is 0 and or_result is the original expression, so no later cycle is touched. bgm's reset port is unused by the design (only bgm.v:6/20), so the block's reset is never asserted again and the testbench's mid-run resets change nothing.

Blocks and tiles. 11 blocks, one per fpu_mul, each taking a DSP tile alone: 11 DSP tiles, the same count as the baseline, which already placed these 11 multiplies on tiles. The gain is not the tile count but the 1056 soft flip-flops (96 per instance: prod1 and prod) that the block's two register stages absorb, about 106 BLEs' worth per instance.

Stages and delay. No block is chained to another: the longest series of blocks in any stage is one, and a stage that lies inside a block is 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, far under the critical path. Two stages change:

  • The stage ending at the old prod1 (opa_r -> fracta_mul -> adder-tree multiply -> flip-flop) is replaced by opa_r -> fracta_mul -> block input register, ~0.06 + wire + 0.019 setup; it was not the slowest stage and is now much shorter.
  • The slowest stage of the design is unchanged in content: prod -> post_norm (pri_encoder, the left shifter, the fract_out+1 round adder, bgm.v:1300/1405/1339) -> out_o1, 19.5909 ns in the baseline and 19.684 ns as measured on the kept candidate, where it started at the soft prod flip-flop (clock-to-Q 0.060 plus a 0.660 ns wire). It now starts at the block's result register instead: 0.060 clock-to-Q plus a "block into soft logic" wire (0.54-1.90, median 0.96), i.e. about +0.3 ns at the median, giving ~19.9 ns. That is the STAGE_NS above; it is the slowest stage, and the rewrite makes no other stage longer than it. This is the one place the plan goes against the cost guidance: it lengthens the slowest stage by roughly 1.5% to take 1056 flip-flops (about -2.9% of used logic area on top of the -2.85% already measured for the first pass) out of the design, and it drops the first pass's extra routing of the raw 32-bit opa/opb into the DSP column (the first pass's +1.43% wirelength) by feeding the blocks from the local fracta_mul/fractb_mul again. At the median wire the product lands near 0.94 x 1.02 x ~1.01 ~ 0.97 of the baseline, below the kept 0.990.

Soft logic leaving each stage. Out of the fracta_mul -> prod1 stage: the 24x24 multiply (it leaves the one_mult_27x27 DSP for the block) and the 48-bit prod1 register. Out of the prod1 -> prod stage: that stage disappears entirely with the 48-bit prod register. Added soft logic: one extra literal (~fpu_op[1]) on the existing or_result LUT and one reset net per block; nothing is added to the 48-bit product path or to the slowest stage.

Register stages, before and after. Before: operands fracta_mul/fractb_mul to the product prod = 2 register stages, both soft (prod1, prod), and prod is read combinationally by fract_denorm/post_norm in the next stage. After: the same 2 register stages, both inside the block (operand register, result register), 0 soft; the product is read directly off result[47:0] in the same cycle the original read prod. Latency from opa/opb to prod is 3 edges before and after.

Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle, on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_bgm.v) and the block's behavioural model (mult_add_int_27x27.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

response

I'll start by reading the design and the relevant regions.

Now making the three edits.

I'll verify the rewrite by simulation locally. First, reconstruct the original and a trace harness.

Done. check.sh passes with 11 mult_add_int_27x27 instances and no $mul cells.

Changes, region by region

Region mul_r2.$mul$bgm.v:1174$336(sum_in=fpu_mul) — converted exactly as the plan specifies, in three places:

  1. mul_r2 (bgm.v:1166-1185) — gained a clr input; both always @(posedge clk) blocks and reg [47:0] prod1, prod removed. The module now instantiates mult_add_int_27x27 u0 with clk<-clk, reset<-clr, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={3'b0, opa}, ay={3'b0, opb}, bx=36'b0, chainin=64'b0, scanin=27'b0, chainout/scanout unconnected, and assign prod = result[47:0]. prod is now a wire. The block's two register stages stand exactly where prod1 and prod stood, so latency from opa/opb to prod is unchanged; both operands are unsigned 24-bit and zero-extend into the 27-bit ports, so the 48-bit product is exact and result[63:48] is always 0.
  2. bgm.v:695 — mul_r2 u5 now passes .clr(~fpu_op[1]). fpu_op is 0 before the first clock edge and 3'b010 after every edge (bgm.v:593-595), so the block is cleared at edge 1 only, reproducing the original's two leading zeros on prod, and is never cleared again (bgm's own reset port is unused by the design).
  3. bgm.v:734 — assign or_result = mul_00 | div_00 | ~fpu_op[1]; forces post_norm's output_zero high in the single pre-first-edge window where the block's result is X but the original's prod was 0, so the five *_o1 registers latch the original's values at edge 1.

No other reader changed: fract_denorm (bgm.v:719-720) and (prod!=48'b0) (bgm.v:843) still read prod as before.

Verification. Beyond check.sh, I ran the reference stimulus (sim_reference/tb_equiv_bgm.v, same reset pattern, impulse probe and 4000 random cycles, with every soft register zero-initialized and the block model's registers left at X) against both the original and the rewritten design with iverilog, comparing Fn_out on all 4121 compared cycles: identical for seeds 0x2545f491, 0xdeadbeef and 0x00000001, with no X on the rewritten design's output at any compared cycle.

No assumptions about the design's inputs are needed.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

}

// mult_add_int_27x27: result = ax*ay + bx + chainin, unsigned, the product and bx zero-extended
// to 64 bits, modulo 2^64 (the sum wraps at the result width).
// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[27], ay[27], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[27] in;
// result[64], chainout[64], scanout[27] out. mode_sigs and scanin are not read (the scan chain is
// unused and tied to 0 in every spec). bx is 36 bits, the pb_type's port width (the arch header's
// "27x27 plus 64" names an addend the tile's data pins cannot carry).
// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx and chainin alike); the product and
// the sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
// Cycles: ax, ay, bx and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (1 cycle from the
// inputs).
// scanout is the ay register (ay to scanout 1), as in mult_add_int_18x19; nothing specifies it.
// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
// X until the first clear.
module mult_add_int_27x27 (
    input clk,
    input reset,
    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
    input [26:0] ax,
    input [26:0] ay,
    input [35:0] bx,
    input [63:0] chainin,
    input [26:0] scanin,
    output reg [63:0] result,
    output [63:0] chainout,
    output [26:0] scanout
);

    // stage 1: the input registers
    reg [26:0] ax_reg;
    reg [26:0] ay_reg;
    reg [35:0] bx_reg;
    reg [63:0] chainin_reg;

    // combinational middle: the product, the addend and chainin, from the registers
    wire [53:0] prod = ax_reg * ay_reg;                          // 27x27 -> 54, exact
    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx_reg + chainin_reg;              // zero-extended, modulo 2^64

    always @(posedge clk) begin
        if (reset) begin
            result <= 0;
            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
        end
        else begin
            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; chainin_reg <= chainin;
            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
        end
    end

    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
    assign scanout  = ay_reg;

endmodule
=====

/*
always @(posedge clk)
begin
---
assign   expa = opa[30:23];
assign   expb = opb[30:23];

////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
//
// Calculate Exponenet
//

assign expa_dn   = !(|expa);
assign expb_dn   = !(|expb);
assign opa_00    = !(|opa[30:0]);
assign opb_00    = !(|opb[30:0]);

assign fractb_00 = !(|opb[22:0]);

assign fractb[22:0] = opb[22:0];
assign fracta[23:23] = !expa_dn;
assign fractb[23:23] = !expb_dn;

assign {co1,exp_tmp1} = op_div ? ({1'b0,expa[7:0]} - {1'b0,expb[7:0]}) : ({1'b0,expa[7:0]} + {1'b0,expb[7:0]});
assign {co2,exp_tmp2} = op_div ? ({co1,exp_tmp1} + 9'h07f) : ({co1,exp_tmp1} - 9'h07f);
assign exp_tmp3 = exp_tmp2 + 8'h01;
assign exp_tmp4 = 8'h7f - exp_tmp1;

// on 2026-10-22; do not edit, regenerate (the command is in make_testbenches.sh):

// Compares every output every cycle: Fn_out[32].

    // one xorshift32 PRNG behind every random value; draw sets r32

    task draw; begin prng = prng ^ (prng << 13); prng = prng ^ (prng >> 17); prng = prng ^ (prng << 5); r32 = prng; end endtask
    // bookkeeping
    localparam NOUT = 1; localparam MAXW = 32;

    reg [MAXW-1:0] prev [0:NOUT-1];
    integer n = 0, cycle = 0, same = 0, delsum = 0, nrec = 0, k;
    reg cmp = 0; reg [8*16-1:0] phase = "init";
    integer rec_cycle [0:9]; integer rec_out [0:9]; reg [MAXW-1:0] rec_exp [0:9], rec_act [0:9]; reg [8*16-1:0] rec_phase [0:9]; time rec_t [0:9];
    function [8*40-1:0] oname; input integer j; begin
        case (j)
            0: oname = "Fn_out";

        endcase
    end endfunction
    function [31:0] owidth; input integer j; begin
        case (j)
            0: owidth = 32;
            default: owidth = 0;
        endcase
    end endfunction
    task note_mismatch; input integer j; input [MAXW-1:0] e, a; begin
        same = same + 1; mm[j] = mm[j] + 1;
        if (nrec < 10) begin rec_cycle[nrec] = cycle; rec_out[nrec] = j; rec_exp[nrec] = e; rec_act[nrec] = a; rec_phase[nrec] = phase; rec_t[nrec] = $time; nrec = nrec + 1; end
        if (nrec == 1 && mm[j] == 1 && same == 1) $display("first divergence: cycle=%0d t=%0t phase=%0s output=%0s ref=%0h dut=%0h", cycle, $time, phase, oname(j), e, a);
    end endtask
    // the compare, at the negedge before the stimulus of the next cycle is applied
    task check; begin
        cycle = cycle + 1;
        if (cmp) begin
            n = n + 1;
            if (Fn_out_dut !== Fn_out_ref) note_mismatch(0, Fn_out_ref, Fn_out_dut);
            if (Fn_out_dut !== prev[0][31:0]) begin delsum = delsum + 1; dl[0] = dl[0] + 1; end

        end
        prev[0] = Fn_out_ref;
    end endtask
    // one cycle: compare, then the caller sets the next inputs
    task tick; begin @(negedge clock); check; end endtask
    task set_data;   // every free input random over its full width; controls by their probability
    begin

        draw; dw_z[31:0] = r32[31:0];
        draw; dt[31:0] = r32[31:0];
    end endtask
    task set_zero; begin   // the zero phases: every free input 0, controls at their idle value
        sigma_a = 0;
        sigma_b = 0;
        sigma_c = 0;
        Fn = 0;
        dw_x = 0;
        dw_y = 0;
        dw_z = 0;
        dt = 0;
    end endtask
    task resets_on; begin reset = 1'b1; end endtask
    task resets_off; begin reset = 1'b0; end endtask
    task resets_random; begin
        draw; reset = (`MIDRUN_RESETS && (r32 % `RESET_RATE) == 0) ? 1'b1 : 1'b0;
    end endtask
    task set_random; begin set_data; resets_random; end endtask
    task rand_cycles; input integer m; integer q; begin for (q = 0; q < m; q = q + 1) begin tick; set_data; resets_off; end end endtask

prompt
Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_27x27 (precision INT27)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[27], ay[27], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[27]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[27]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The design

The file bgm.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • The edit does what the approved plan says; any change outside the regions it names is explained by the plan.
  • Every block input port is connected, and the block's outputs drive what the plan says they drive.
  • The correctness rules hold for the edited source.

The synthesis checks and the reference simulation have passed; the remaining checks run after your review.

  • If REJECT: the rule, the lines, what is wrong.

TILES: 11
STAGE_NS: 19.9
MAPPING: per block (one per mul_r2 instance u5, i.e. one per fpu_mul, 11 of them): clk <- clk (bgm's single clock, bgm.v:101 etc.); reset <- clr, a new 1-bit input port of mul_r2 driven in fpu_mul by ~fpu_op[1] (fpu_op is the design's own register, bgm.v:536/593-595); mode_sigs <- 12'b0; ax <- {3'b0, opa} where mul_r2.opa keeps its existing driver fracta_mul (pre_norm_fmul's combinational fraction output, bgm.v:666/695/1100-1103); ay <- {3'b0, opb} = {3'b0, fractb_mul}; bx <- 36'b0; chainin <- 64'b0; scanin <- 27'b0; result[47:0] -> prod, by assign prod = result[47:0] (both soft registers prod1 and prod of bgm.v:1171-1177 are removed; prod becomes a wire); result[63:48] unused (a 24x24 product never reaches bit 48); chainout, scanout left unconnected. prod keeps driving fract_denorm (bgm.v:719-720) and (prod!=48'b0) (bgm.v:843) unchanged; in fpu_mul, or_result becomes mul_00 | div_00 | ~fpu_op[1] (bgm.v:734).

PLAN:

Candidate regions. There is one candidate, mul_r2.$mul$bgm.v:1174$336(sum_in=fpu_mul), 11 instances. It is converted; nothing is left.

What changes against the unmodified source (this plan is applied to bgm.v as given, not to the kept candidate).

  1. mul_r2 (bgm.v:1166-1179) keeps its 24-bit opa/opb ports and its instantiation operands (fracta_mul, fractb_mul), gains one input clr, and loses both always @(posedge clk) blocks and the reg [47:0] prod1, prod:
module mul_r2(clk, clr, opa, opb, prod);
input		clk;
input		clr;			// clears the block at the first clock edge
input	[23:0]	opa, opb;
output	[47:0]	prod;

wire	[63:0]	result;

mult_add_int_27x27 u0(
		.clk(clk), .reset(clr), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
		.ax({3'b0, opa}), .ay({3'b0, opb}), .bx(36'b0),
		.chainin(64'b0), .scanin(27'b0),
		.result(result), .chainout(), .scanout()
		);

assign prod = result[47:0];
endmodule
  1. bgm.v:695 becomes mul_r2 u5(.clk(clk), .clr(~fpu_op[1]), .opa(fracta_mul), .opb(fractb_mul), .prod(prod));
  2. bgm.v:734 becomes assign or_result = mul_00 | div_00 | ~fpu_op[1];

Why this is the same behaviour.

Latency. In the original the chain from the module input opa is opa_r (bgm.v:597-598) -> prod1 (1174) -> prod (1177): three register edges, with fracta_mul combinational from opa_r. After the rewrite the chain is opa_r -> block operand register -> block result register: the block's two registers stand exactly where prod1 and prod stood, the operands presented to the block are the same fracta_mul/fractb_mul on the same edges, so prod after edge k is the product of fracta_mul,fractb_mul sampled before edge k-1, as before. Both operands are unsigned 24-bit wires zero-extended into the 27-bit ports, the 48-bit product is exact and never reaches result[48], so result[47:0] is bit-for-bit the original opa*opb. No term of the original arithmetic is dropped: bx and chainin have no counterpart and are tied to 0, as the block's contract allows.

Start-up. The original's prod is 0 after edge 1 (it loads prod1's power-up 0) and 0 after edge 2 (prod1 loaded fracta_mul*fractb_mul with opa_r=opb_r=0, and fracta_mul = {!expa_dn, opa_r[22:0]} = 0 for a zero operand, bgm.v:1093/1102). fpu_op is 0 before the first edge and 3'b010 after every edge (bgm.v:593-595), so ~fpu_op[1] is high only at edge 1: the block clears all of its registers there, giving result=0 after edge 1, and after edge 2 it loads the product of the cleared operand registers, 0 — the same two zeros. From edge 3 on the block is in its steady state and matches the original. Nothing relies on a power-up value of a register the rewrite adds; the rewrite adds no register.

The one X window. Between time 0 and the first clock edge the block's result is X (the block has no initial value), whereas the original's prod is 0 there, and that window is sampled once, at edge 1, by out_o1, ine_o1, overflow_o1, inf_o1 and zero_o1 through post_norm. Forcing or_result (i.e. post_norm.output_zero) high in exactly that window reproduces the original's values there, without any gate in the 48-bit datapath: with the design's registers at their power-up 0, post_norm sees fpu_op=0, exp_in=0, exp_ovf=0, rmode=0, opa_dn=opb_dn=0, so dn=1 (bgm.v:1330, exp_in_00=1), op_mul=op_div=op_f2i=op_i2f=0 and max_num=0 (bgm.v:1550, rmode_00=1). In the original with fract_in=0 this yields out=31'h0 (shift left by 2 of zero, no rounding, exp_out=0), ine=0, overflow=0, underflow=0; in the rewrite with fract_in=X and output_zero=1, exp_out_final and fract_out_final are forced to 0 (bgm.v:1596/1600) so out=31'h0 and out_d_00=1, ine reduces to max_num=0 (bgm.v:1672-1674, dn=1 kills the r/s terms), overflow = ovf0 | ovf1 = 0 (exp_out_final=0; ovf1 is ANDed with !dn=0) and underflow = (!fract_in[47] & exp_out1_co) & !dn = 0. Every value the five registers latch at edge 1 is therefore identical to the original's. The other reader of prod, bgm.v:843, is ANDed with (opa_dn | opb_dn) = 0 in that window, so it is 0 in both. From edge 1 on result is defined, ~fpu_op[1] is 0 and or_result is the original expression, so no later cycle is touched. bgm's reset port is unused by the design (only bgm.v:6/20), so the block's reset is never asserted again and the testbench's mid-run resets change nothing.

Blocks and tiles. 11 blocks, one per fpu_mul, each taking a DSP tile alone: 11 DSP tiles, the same count as the baseline, which already placed these 11 multiplies on tiles. The gain is not the tile count but the 1056 soft flip-flops (96 per instance: prod1 and prod) that the block's two register stages absorb, about 106 BLEs' worth per instance.

Stages and delay. No block is chained to another: the longest series of blocks in any stage is one, and a stage that lies inside a block is 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, far under the critical path. Two stages change:

  • The stage ending at the old prod1 (opa_r -> fracta_mul -> adder-tree multiply -> flip-flop) is replaced by opa_r -> fracta_mul -> block input register, ~0.06 + wire + 0.019 setup; it was not the slowest stage and is now much shorter.
  • The slowest stage of the design is unchanged in content: prod -> post_norm (pri_encoder, the left shifter, the fract_out+1 round adder, bgm.v:1300/1405/1339) -> out_o1, 19.5909 ns in the baseline and 19.684 ns as measured on the kept candidate, where it started at the soft prod flip-flop (clock-to-Q 0.060 plus a 0.660 ns wire). It now starts at the block's result register instead: 0.060 clock-to-Q plus a "block into soft logic" wire (0.54-1.90, median 0.96), i.e. about +0.3 ns at the median, giving ~19.9 ns. That is the STAGE_NS above; it is the slowest stage, and the rewrite makes no other stage longer than it. This is the one place the plan goes against the cost guidance: it lengthens the slowest stage by roughly 1.5% to take 1056 flip-flops (about -2.9% of used logic area on top of the -2.85% already measured for the first pass) out of the design, and it drops the first pass's extra routing of the raw 32-bit opa/opb into the DSP column (the first pass's +1.43% wirelength) by feeding the blocks from the local fracta_mul/fractb_mul again. At the median wire the product lands near 0.94 x 1.02 x ~1.01 ~ 0.97 of the baseline, below the kept 0.990.

Soft logic leaving each stage. Out of the fracta_mul -> prod1 stage: the 24x24 multiply (it leaves the one_mult_27x27 DSP for the block) and the 48-bit prod1 register. Out of the prod1 -> prod stage: that stage disappears entirely with the 48-bit prod register. Added soft logic: one extra literal (~fpu_op[1]) on the existing or_result LUT and one reset net per block; nothing is added to the 48-bit product path or to the slowest stage.

Register stages, before and after. Before: operands fracta_mul/fractb_mul to the product prod = 2 register stages, both soft (prod1, prod), and prod is read combinationally by fract_denorm/post_norm in the next stage. After: the same 2 register stages, both inside the block (operand register, result register), 0 soft; the product is read directly off result[47:0] in the same cycle the original read prod. Latency from opa/opb to prod is 3 edges before and after.

DESIGN_CHANGE: the design's slowest stage is post_norm's normalize-round cone (leading-zero count, 48-bit shift, 24-bit round adder, bgm.v:1300-1484), which contains no multiply and cannot be moved into the block; inserting one pipeline register inside post_norm (e.g. between the shifter and the rounding adder) in all 11 fpu_mul and 9 fpu_add instances would roughly halve the period, after which the block's own 2.219 ns path and the DSP-to-logic wire would still be slack-free and the area won here would convert into a product gain several times larger.

The edit
