Simulation

simulation passed: 4121 cycles, 0 mismatches. The result (sim_result.json) and log (sim_run.log) are attached. Simulation shows the outputs match on the cycles the testbench drives; it does not show that the edit follows the plan or the correctness rules. That is your check.

The testbench (tb_equiv_bgm.v) and the block's behavioural model (mult_add_int_27x27.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: bgm.v, sim_result.json, sim_run.log

response

I'll read the design and the simulation artifacts.

ACCEPT

The edit matches the approved plan exactly, in the three places it names and nowhere else (bgm.v:695, bgm.v:734, bgm.v:1166-1183); mul_r2 is instantiated only at bgm.v:695, so the single edit covers all 11 fpu_mul instances, and fpu_add's post_norm (bgm.v:2233) is untouched.

Checks:

  • Ports. All eight inputs connected by contract name (bgm.v:1174-1179): clk<-clk, reset<-clr, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={3'b0,opa}, ay={3'b0,opb}, bx=36'b0, chainin=64'b0, scanin=27'b0; chainout/scanout left open, so nothing reads chainout outside the dedicated path. Widths match the model in sim_reference/mult_add_int_27x27.v.
  • Terms and arithmetic. Only opa*opb existed; bx and chainin are tied to the constant 0 the contract allows. fracta_mul/fractb_mul are unsigned 24-bit (bgm.v:1100-1103, 1047), so zero-extension to 27 bits is exact and the 48-bit product never reaches result[48]; assign prod = result[47:0] is bit-identical.
  • Latency. Original opa_r->prod1(edge k)->prod(edge k+1) is replaced by the block's operand register and result register in the same two slots; prod after edge k+1 is still the product of the operands presented before edge k.
  • Reset / power-up. clr=~fpu_op[1] is high only before the first edge (fpu_op is 0 at start and 3'b010 at every edge thereafter, bgm.v:593-595), so the block clears at edge 1 and never again. After edge 1 both designs hold prod=0; at edge 2 the original loads prod1=fracta_mul*fractb_mul with opa_r=opb_r=0, which is 0 (bgm.v:1093/1102), and the block loads the product of its cleared operand registers, also 0; states agree from then on. bgm's reset port is unused (only lines 6 and 20), so the testbench's mid-run reset changes neither design and the block's reset is never reasserted. No added register is relied on for an initial value.
  • X window. Between time 0 and edge 1 result is X while the original prod is 0. or_result is forced high in exactly that window, and with fpu_op_r3=0, rmode_r3=0, exp_r=0, exp_ovf_r=0 this makes exp_out_final and fract_out_final constant 0 (bgm.v:1600/1604, inf_out=0, ovf0=0), dn=1 kills the r/s terms of ine (bgm.v:1676-1678) and the ovf1/underflow terms (bgm.v:1496/1659), and op_div/op_mul/op_f2i/op_i2f/max_num are all clean 0 — the same out_d=31'h0, ine_d=overflow_d=underflow_d=0 the original produces with fract_in=0, so the five registers latched at edge 1 are unchanged. The other reader of prod (bgm.v:843) is ANDed with sign=0 in that window, so the X does not escape.
  • Visibility. prod's readers (bgm.v:720, 843) see the same value; prod is now a wire rather than a reg, which no other code depends on. No value that is read elsewhere was moved inside the block.

No ASSUMES: line is needed: the rewrite rests only on the stated power-up-zero rule for the original's registers and on reset being unconnected in the design.

 "file_path": "/work/sim_reference/mult_add_int_27x27.v"

output
1	// mult_add_int_27x27: result = ax*ay + bx + chainin, unsigned, the product and bx zero-extended
2	// to 64 bits, modulo 2^64 (the sum wraps at the result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[27], ay[27], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[27] in;
4	// result[64], chainout[64], scanout[27] out. mode_sigs and scanin are not read (the scan chain is
5	// unused and tied to 0 in every spec). bx is 36 bits, the pb_type's port width (the arch header's
6	// "27x27 plus 64" names an addend the tile's data pins cannot carry).
7	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
8	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx and chainin alike); the product and
9	// the sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
10	// Cycles: ax, ay, bx and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
11	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (1 cycle from the
12	// inputs).
13	// scanout is the ay register (ay to scanout 1), as in mult_add_int_18x19; nothing specifies it.
14	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
15	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
16	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
17	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
18	// X until the first clear.
19	module mult_add_int_27x27 (
20	    input clk,
21	    input reset,
22	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
23	    input [26:0] ax,
24	    input [26:0] ay,
25	    input [35:0] bx,
26	    input [63:0] chainin,
27	    input [26:0] scanin,
28	    output reg [63:0] result,
29	    output [63:0] chainout,
30	    output [26:0] scanout
31	);
32	
33	    // stage 1: the input registers
34	    reg [26:0] ax_reg;
35	    reg [26:0] ay_reg;
36	    reg [35:0] bx_reg;
37	    reg [63:0] chainin_reg;
38	
39	    // combinational middle: the product, the addend and chainin, from the registers
40	    wire [53:0] prod = ax_reg * ay_reg;                          // 27x27 -> 54, exact
41	    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx_reg + chainin_reg;              // zero-extended, modulo 2^64
42	
43	    always @(posedge clk) begin
44	        if (reset) begin
45	            result <= 0;
46	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
47	        end
48	        else begin
49	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; chainin_reg <= chainin;
50	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
51	        end
52	    end
53	
54	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
55	    assign scanout  = ay_reg;
56	
57	endmodule
58

sites converted1sites the approved plan names
site instances converted11converted sites times their module's instance count
blocks packed11packed block instances in the target mode (editor attempt 1)
tiles packed11DSP tiles in the target mode (the .net mode count); absent on records before 2026-10-15

Offered and packed are not the same unit: a site is a source region, a packed block is an elaborated instance, and one block can fuse several sites (or the editor can chain several blocks for one site), so they are recorded raw rather than as a ratio.

Cost a client-side list-price estimate per call, summed, not a bill. Token components are kept separate (uncached input, output, reasoning, cache read, cache write); total is their sum, with reasoning counted once. Reasoning tokens are already counted inside the output column.

Objective loop

2 rewrite(s) measured · stopped by: no gain beyond noise
revisions allowed
9
seeds per rewrite
1, 2, 3, 4, 5 (adaptive)
kept
Pass 1
loop stopped by
no gain beyond noise (gain -2.62% against first, min-gain 0.0125 (baseline adw_reg_seed_spread_frac))
objective
area x delay x wirelength (delay register to register, 2026-10-20)
baseline
layout koios_extra_small · used area 6.35391e+07 · critical path 19.5909 ns · wirelength 395851 · area x delay x wirelength (delay register to register, 2026-10-20) 4.92751e+14 (read from entry; median per term over the entry's seed runs (its seed_medians; seeds lists them))

The objective is area x delay x wirelength (delay register to register, 2026-10-20): VPR's total used logic block area (I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded) times the critical path times the total routed wirelength. Each term is the median over the loop's seeds; the product is the product of the medians. Beside every product stand the ratios against the baseline, A/A0 (area), D/D0 (critical path), W/W0 (wirelength) and their product, so the normalization can be read off. After each measured rewrite, the planner is given every measurement so far and asked for a revision. A revision is built and checked like the first rewrite, and the lowest product is kept, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 1.25%. The loop stops when the planner declines to revise, when a revision does not lower the best product by more than 1.25%, when a revision fails its checks, or when 9 revisions have been made.

▶ Pass 1T2111119.59091119.59676.17253e+074014484.85596e+14-1.45%0.971 · 1.000 · 1.014—no hard block and no multiply on path 1not recordedkept
Pass 2T2111119.91119.8376.30089e+074005105.006e+14+1.59%0.992 · 1.013 · 1.012+3.09%block n373021abc: ax 27-bit signal, ay 27-bit signal, bx 0, chainin 0, chainout 64-bit signal, mode_sigs 0, result 64-bit signal, scanin 0, scanout 27-bit signalnot recorded

Stated: the BLOCKS, TILES and STAGE_NS lines of the plan header. Measured: the packed DSP tile count from the gate flow, and the median period, area and wirelength over the seeds. "A/A0 · D/D0 · W/W0" are the rewrite's medians over the baseline's, whose product is the "vs baseline" change plus one. "vs previous" is against the rewrite measured before this one. "Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants. Verification comes from verification.yaml in the run dir when one was written; the loop itself does not run the equivalence checks. Its label is proven when the LEC closed, simulation under assumption when the simulation passed without a closed proof and the plan stated a bound it rests on, simulation only when it stated none; the hover text gives the simulation and LEC results and the stated bounds.
